Lemma 11.10

(Delay coordinate functor )
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
and
2
hold, and
k\in\mathbb{N}
is any integer. Then there is a functor

\text{Delay}^{(k)}:\textbf{\text{DSM}}\to\textbf{\text{DSM}},\quad(\Omega,\Phi,\phi)\mapsto(\Omega,\Phi,\phi^{(k)}).

(34)

whose action on a morphism
(h,A):(\Omega,\Phi,\phi)\to(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
is
(h,A^{k})
. Moreover, there is a natural transformation
\alpha^{(k)}:\text{Delay}^{(k)}\to\Id
.

The proof of Lemma
11.10
requires the following elementary observation :

Lemma 11.11
.

Let
\Context
be as in Assumption
1
and
A
be an object. For each
n
and
k
, there is a morphism
r_{A,n,k}:(A^{k+1})^{n+1}\to A^{n+k+1}
such that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A^{k+1})^{n+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{n+k+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{k+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{A,n,k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{i}}

for
i=0,1,\cdots,k
and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A^{k+1})^{n+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{n+k+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{k+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{A,n,k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{i-k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{k}}

for
i=k+1,\cdots,k+n
.

Proof of Lemma
11.10

The action on morphisms can be verified from this diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota Y)^{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota Y^{\prime})^{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota Y^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.3672,0.0742}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{(k)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{A}_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi^{\prime i}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.3672,0.0742}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{\prime(k)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{A^{\prime}}_{i}}

The compositionality of this action is trivial. We construct a family of morphisms

\alpha^{(k)}_{n}:\Data(\Omega,\Phi,\phi^{(k)})_{n}\to\Data(\Omega,\Phi,\phi)_{n+k}

that commutes with
\mathfrak{f}
and
\eta
. Observe that

r_{A,n,k}\circ\pi_{\mathbb{N}\to n+1}\circ\xi_{(\Omega,\Phi,\phi^{(k)})}=\pi_{\mathbb{N}\to n+k+1}\circ\xi_{(\Omega,\Phi,\phi)}

by a direct calculation. This implies that we have the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota Y^{k+1})^{n+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota Y^{n+k+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathbb{N}\to n+1}\circ\xi_{(\Omega,\Phi,\phi^{(k)})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathbb{N}\to n+k+1}\circ\xi_{(\Omega,\Phi,\phi)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{n}}

From Lemma
5.2
and the definition of
\Data
, we obtain a morphism
\alpha^{(k)}_{n}:\Data(\Omega,\Phi,\phi^{(k)})_{n}\to\Data(\Omega,\Phi,\phi)_{n+k}
. By considering the underlying set structure, we can check that it commutes with
f
and
\eta
. This completes the proof of Lemma
11.10
. ∎

The main utility of Lemma
11.10
is in showing that delay-coordinates may be used to convert jump-
k
morphisms into jump-
0
morphisms.

Lemma 11.12

(Delay coordinated data)
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
and
2
hold, and
\psi:\Data(\Omega,\Phi,\phi)\to X
be a TSD morphism of jump
k
. Then, there exists a TSD morphism
\psi^{(k)}:\Data(\Omega,\Phi,\phi^{(k)})\to X
of jump
0
.

Proof.

The composition
\psi_{n+k}\circ\alpha^{(k)}_{n}:\Data(\Omega,\Phi,\phi^{(k)})_{n}\to X_{n}
gives the desired TSD morphism of jump 0. ∎

11.10  Reconstruction

Lemma 11.13
.

Let
\psi:\Data(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\to\Data\circ\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}_{obs}(\Omega,\Phi)
be a TSD morphism of jump
0
. Then, there is a morphism
h:(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime})\to(\Omega,\Phi)
in
\mathrm{DS}
.

Proof.

From the definition of
\Data
,
\psi
is determined uniquely by the following morphism
\psi_{0}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Data(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Data(\Omega,\Phi,1_{\Omega})_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota Y^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{0}}

Note that a morphism
h:\Omega^{\prime}\to\iota\Omega
in
\Context
emerges from this diagram. Now we check that
h
is a semiconjugacy. By Property
6
it is sufficient to show that
U(\Phi\circ h)=U(h\circ\Phi^{\prime})
. This allows the analysis to be conducted in
Set
. We begin by noting that :

Uh(\omega^{\prime})=U(\psi_{0}\circ\phi^{\prime})(\omega^{\prime}),\quad\forall\omega^{\prime}\in U(\Omega^{\prime}),

by construction. Now consider the set of length-
2
sequences
U\left(\Data(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})_{1}\right)
Pick a typical element
\left(U\phi^{\prime}(\omega^{\prime}),U(\phi^{\prime}\circ\Phi^{\prime})(\omega^{\prime})\right)
from here. Then there must be an
\omega\in\Omega
such that

\left(\omega,U\Phi(\omega)\right)=\left(U\psi_{1}\right)\left(U\phi^{\prime}(\omega^{\prime}),U(\phi^{\prime}\circ\Phi^{\prime})(\omega^{\prime})\right)\quad\in U\circ\Data\circ\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}_{obs}(\Omega,\Phi).

By applying
\eta_{1}
to both sides of the equality, we obtain

\omega=U\psi_{0}\circ U\phi^{\prime}(\omega^{\prime}).

and if we apply
f_{1}
, we obtain

U\Phi(\omega)=U\psi_{0}\circ U(\phi^{\prime}\circ\Phi^{\prime})(\omega^{\prime}).

Together they imply

U(\Phi\circ h)(\omega^{\prime})=U(h\circ\Phi^{\prime})(\omega^{\prime}).

As
\omega^{\prime}
is arbitrary, this implies
U(\Phi\circ h)=U(h\circ\Phi^{\prime})
as claimed.
∎

Proof of Theorem
7

We begin by drawing a large commutation diagram :

DSM
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\textbf{\text{DS}}_{obs}}
DSMO
DS
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\TSD}
DS
DS
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.3672,0.0742}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Data^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.0586,0.3672,0.0742}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Forget^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}_{obs}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.99,0.27,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subset}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.99,0.27,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime\prime}_{obs}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{obs}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.5664,0.1484,0.6523}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Data}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.5664,0.1484,0.6523}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Forget}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.5664,0.1484,0.6523}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Lan}_{\Data}\Forget}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.99,0.27,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.99,0.27,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

The commutations upheld by the red arrows are to be verified. The
\Data
and
\Forget
functors from
DSMO
have been pulled back along
\kappa
to create analogous functors
\Data^{\prime}
and
\Forget^{\prime}
functors respectively from
DSM
. Note that the functor
\kappa
has a right inverse. Then by Lemma
11.4
(iii) we have

\mathrm{Lan}_{\Data}{\Forget}=\mathrm{Lan}_{\Data\kappa}{\Forget\kappa}=\mathrm{Lan}_{\Data^{\prime}}{\Forget^{\prime}}.

This indicates that in analyzing the Kan construction, we are free to base the construction around
DSMO
or
DSM
. We choose the latter for convenience. Thus the goal now is to prove

\left(\mathrm{Lan}_{\Data^{\prime}}{\Forget^{\prime}}\right)\circ\Data^{\prime}\circ\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}_{obs}=\iota^{\prime\prime}_{obs}.

Fix an
(\Omega,\Phi)
in
\textbf{\text{DS}}_{obs}
. By the colimit formula of left Kan extension, we have

\mathrm{Lan}_{\Data^{\prime}}{\Forget^{\prime}}\left(\Data^{\prime}\circ\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}_{obs}(\Omega,\Phi)\right)=\mathrm{colim}\,\{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime}):\Data(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\to\Data\circ\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}_{obs}(\Omega,\Phi)\}.

(35)

Thus the colimit is over a certain collection
(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime})
of dynamical systems which are a part of some
DSM
object which yield a data object
X^{\prime}
on the left slice of
X
. Our goal is to show that the colimit is the original dynamical system
\iota^{\prime}_{obs}\left(\Omega,\Phi\right)
. For simplicity of notation we denote this object as
\left(\Omega,\Phi\right)
.

First we check that there is a cocone with vertex
(\Omega,\Phi)
. We recall that the colimit in equation (
35
) is taken over the slice category
(\Data\downarrow\Data\circ\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}_{obs}(\Omega,\Phi))
. An object in this category is a pair of a
DSM
object
(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
and a
\TSD
morphism
\psi:\Data(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\to\Data\circ\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}_{obs}(\Omega,\Phi)
. A morphism
((\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi)\to((\Omega^{\prime\prime},\Phi^{\prime\prime},\phi^{\prime\prime}),\psi^{\prime})
is a
DSM
morphism
(h,A):(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\to(\Omega^{\prime\prime},\Phi^{\prime\prime},\phi^{\prime\prime})
such that

\hbox to319.11pt{\vbox to57.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 159.55397pt\lower-26.3373pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{56.99017pt}{-8.14835pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 78.86 -11.27)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

Lemmas
11.12
and
11.13
guarantee that we have the following semi-conjugacies of dynamics

\gamma:=\gamma_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi}:(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime})\to\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}_{obs}(\Omega,\Phi),\quad\gamma^{\prime}:=\gamma_{(\Omega^{\prime\prime},\Phi^{\prime\prime},\phi^{\prime\prime}),\psi^{\prime}}:(\Omega^{\prime\prime},\Phi^{\prime\prime})\to\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}_{obs}(\Omega,\Phi)

Now suppose that
\psi
has jump
k
. The semi-conjugacies above are transported by
U
in semi-conjugacies in
Set
-valued dynamics. Combining with the
DSM
-morphism
(h,A)
we get :

\begin{split}U\gamma_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi}(\omega^{\prime})&=U(\psi_{0}^{(k)}\circ\phi^{\prime(k)})(\omega^{\prime})\\
&=\left(U{\psi^{\prime}}_{0}^{(k)}\right)\circ\left(UA_{*}\right)\circ\left(U\phi^{\prime(k)}\right)(\omega^{\prime})\\
&=\left(U{\psi^{\prime}}_{0}^{(k)}\right)\circ\left(U{\phi^{\prime\prime}}^{(k)}\right)\circ\left(Uh\right)(\omega^{\prime})\\
&=\left(U\gamma_{(\Omega^{\prime\prime},\Phi^{\prime\prime},\phi^{\prime\prime}),\psi}\right)\circ\left(Uh\right)(\omega^{\prime}),\end{split}

for every
\omega^{\prime}\in\Omega^{\prime}
. Therefore we have
\gamma_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi}=\gamma_{(\Omega^{\prime\prime},\Phi^{\prime\prime},\phi^{\prime\prime}),\psi}\circ h
in
\mathrm{DS}
. Thus,
\gamma_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi}
is a cocone with vertex
(\Omega,\Phi)
.

Next we check that any cocone
\alpha
with vertex
W
is factorized by
\gamma
when the object
((\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi)
has jump 0:

\alpha_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi}=v\circ\gamma_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi},

where the morphism
v:W\to(\Omega,\Phi)
does not depend on the choice of
((\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi)
and determined uniquely.
Since we have
\TSD
morphism
1:\Data\circ\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}_{obs}(\Omega,\Phi)\to\Data\circ\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}_{obs}(\Omega,\Phi)
, there is
\alpha_{(\Omega,\Phi,1_{\Omega}),1}:(\Omega,\Phi)\to W
. We show that

\alpha_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi}=\alpha_{(\Omega,\Phi,1_{\Omega}),1}\circ\gamma_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi}

for all
((\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi)
with jump 0.
Here we note that, if
\psi:\Data(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})\to\Data\circ\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}_{obs}(\Omega,\Phi)
is a morphism of jump
0
, the morphism

\tilde{\psi}_{0}:\Data(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},{\phi^{\prime}})_{0}=\mathrm{im}\,{\phi^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\psi_{0}}\Data\circ\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}_{obs}(\Omega,\Phi)_{0}\hookrightarrow\Omega

satisfies

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Data(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},{\phi^{\prime}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Data\circ\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}_{obs}(\Omega,\Phi)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Data\circ\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}_{obs}(\Omega,\Phi)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Data(\gamma_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi},\tilde{\psi}_{0})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}

(36)

This is shown as follows. First we check that
(\gamma_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi},\tilde{\psi}_{0})
is a
DSM
morphism from
(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},{\phi^{\prime}})
to
(\Omega,\Phi,1_{\Omega})
. This amounts to verifying that
\gamma_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})}=\tilde{\psi}_{0}\circ{\phi^{\prime}},
but it follows readily from the definition of
\gamma_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})}
.
Next we show that
U\psi_{i}=U\Data(\gamma_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi},\tilde{\psi}_{0})_{i}
for all
i
by induction. The case
i=0
follows from the definition. Now we assume the claim holds for
i-1
. Let
(\xi_{0},\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{i})
be an element in
U\Data(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},{\phi^{\prime}})_{i}
and set

U\psi_{i}(\xi_{0},\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{i})=(\xi^{\prime}_{0},\xi^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\xi^{\prime}_{i}).

Application of
Uf_{i}
shows that

Uf_{i}\circ U\psi_{i}(\xi_{0},\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{i})=U\psi_{i-1}(\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{i})=(U\tilde{\psi}_{0}(\xi_{1}),\cdots,U\tilde{\psi}_{0}(\xi_{i}))

Similarly, application of
U\eta_{i}
shows that

U\eta_{i}\circ U\psi_{i}(\xi_{0},\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{i})=U\psi_{i-1}(\xi_{0},\cdots,\xi_{i-1})=(U\tilde{\psi}_{0}(\xi_{0}),\cdots,U\tilde{\psi}_{0}(\xi_{i-1}))

Combining these result, we conclude

(\xi^{\prime}_{0},\xi^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\xi^{\prime}_{i})=U\Data(\gamma_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi},\tilde{\psi}_{0})_{i}(\xi_{0},\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{i}).

Thus, we have
\psi_{i}=\Data(\gamma_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi},\tilde{\psi}_{0})_{i}
for all
i
.
From the diagram (
36
), we have

\alpha_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},{\phi^{\prime}}),\psi}=\alpha_{(\Omega,\Phi,1_{\Omega}),1}\circ\gamma_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi},

which is the desired factorization.

Now, consider the case where
\alpha
is the universal cocone defining the colimit (
35
). In what follows, we will write
L:=\mathrm{Lan}_{\Data}\mathrm{Frgt}
.

Let the vertex of
\alpha
be
L\circ\iota_{obs}(\Omega,\Phi)
.The cocone
\gamma
can be factorized as

\gamma_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi}=u\circ\alpha_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi},

where
u:L\circ\iota_{obs}(\Omega,\Phi)\to(\Omega,\Phi)
. As
((\Omega,\Phi,1_{\Omega}),1)
has jump 0, we obtain

1_{\Omega}=\gamma_{(\Omega,\Phi,1_{\Omega}),1}=u\circ v\circ\gamma_{(\Omega,\Phi,1_{\Omega}),1}=u\circ v.

Conversely, we obtain

\alpha_{(\Omega,\Phi,1_{\Omega}),1}=v\circ u\circ\alpha_{(\Omega,\Phi,1_{\Omega}),1}.

From the universal property, we have
v\circ u=1_{L\circ\iota_{obs}(\Omega,\Phi)}
. Thus,
u:L\circ\iota_{obs}(\Omega,\Phi)\to(\Omega,\Phi)
is an isomorphism. As
u
depends only on
(\Omega,\Phi)
, we will write
u_{(\Omega,\Phi)}
.

Finally, we verify
u_{(\Omega,\Phi)}
gives a natural transformation
L\circ I\Rightarrow\mathrm{Frgt}\circ I
. Let
h:(\Omega,\Phi)\to(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\Phi})
be a morphism in
\mathrm{DS}_{obs}
. By definition of
L
, we have

\tilde{\alpha}_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),(\Data\circ\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}_{obs}h)\psi}=((L\circ I)h)\circ\alpha_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi},

where
\alpha
and
\tilde{\alpha}
are cocones that define colimits
(L\circ I)(\Omega,\Phi)
and
(L\circ I)(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\Phi})
, respectively.

If we construct a cocone
\tilde{\gamma}
analogously to
\gamma
, we have

\tilde{\gamma}_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),(\Data\circ\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}_{obs}h)\psi}=h\circ\gamma_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi}

by definition. In particular, we have

u_{(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\Phi})}\circ\tilde{\alpha}_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),(\Data\circ\iota^{\prime\prime\prime}_{obs}h)\psi}=h\circ u_{(\Omega,\Phi)}\circ\alpha_{(\Omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}),\psi}.

By the universality of
\alpha
, we have
u_{(\tilde{\Omega},\tilde{\Phi})}\circ((L\circ I)h)=h\circ u_{(\Omega,\Phi)}
, completing the proof of Theorem
4
. ∎
